People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I really hope I should have come in sooner, please don't be mad at me for waiting. I just kept thinking it would go away if I worked harder or got more sleep, you know? I just didn't want to make a fuss or cause any trouble for anyone, I promise.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt unusually depressed or unusually high/energized?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. Um... well, yes. I guess I have had times like that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound like a difficult person, because I truly try to be easy to get along with. 

But sometimes... I just get this huge burst of energy. Like, I'll be flying so high, and I genuinely feel like I could fix every single car in town, all by myself. It feels good at first, really productive! I want to be the best mechanic they have.

I'm not trying to hide anything from you, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your husband is actually trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I don't know if \"harm\" is the right word, and I really don't want to make him sound like a bad person. He's usually great, I promise. It's just... oh gosh, I know this sounds so far-fetched, and I'm really embarrassed to even say it out loud... please don't think I'm completely losing my mind. \n\nBut I'm just still so convinced that he and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track everything I'm doing. They've even sat me down and showed me the camera footage to try and prove there's nothing going on, but... I don't know, even when they say there's nothing there and show me the screens, I'm still convinced they're doing it. \n\nDoes that make me sound terrible for doubting my husband? I really hope you don't judge me for thinking that, I just... I can't shake the feeling, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voice and feeling sure that your husband and the shop owner are tracking you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been going on for a little while now. I really hope I should have come in sooner, please don't be mad at me for waiting. I just kept thinking it would go away if I worked harder or got more sleep, you know? But... gosh, I guess it's been about ten months now. Both the voices and the feeling that they're tracking me. Ten months... does that sound like a really long time to let this go on? I just didn't want to make a fuss or cause any trouble for anyone, I promise.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt unusually depressed or unusually high/energized?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. And I definitely haven't been acting... you know, wild or overly energized. I just try to do my job and be a good wife. I hope that's the right answer? I really haven't noticed anything like that, I promise I'm not hiding anything. I'm not trying to hide anything from you, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your husband is actually trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I don't know if \"harm\" is the right word, and I really don't want to make him sound like a bad person. He's usually great, I promise. It's just... oh gosh, I know this sounds so far-fetched, and I'm really embarrassed to even say it out loud... please don't think I'm completely losing my mind. \n\nBut I'm just still so convinced that he and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track everything I'm doing. They've even sat me down and showed me the camera footage to try and prove there's nothing going on, but... I don't know, even when they say there's nothing there and show me the screens, I'm still convinced they're doing it. \n\nDoes that make me sound terrible for doubting my husband? I really hope you don't judge me for thinking that, I just... I can't shake the feeling, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voice and feeling sure that your husband and the shop owner are tracking you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been going on for a little while now. I really hope I should have come in sooner, please don't be mad at me for waiting. I just kept thinking it would go away if I worked harder or got more sleep, you know? But... gosh, I guess it's been about ten months now. Both the voices and the feeling that they're tracking me. Ten months... does that sound like a really long time to let this go on? I just didn't want to make a fuss or cause any trouble for anyone, I promise.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt unusually depressed or unusually high/energized?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I've just been working at the shop, trying to keep up with everything. I haven't had any times where I couldn't get out of bed for weeks or anything like that. And I definitely haven't been acting... you know, wild or overly energized. I just try to do my job and be a good wife. I hope that's the right answer? I really haven't noticed anything like that, I promise I'm not hiding anything. Is it normal that I just feel... steady, mostly, besides the other stuff?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your thoughts becoming hard to follow or your speech becoming jumbled or disorganized?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I really try hard to speak clearly and make sense when I'm talking to people at the shop, or to my husband. I definitely wouldn't want to confuse anyone or sound... you know, strange. \n\nSometimes I might get a little distracted by... well, by the voices, or worrying about the cameras, but I really do my best to stay on track. I don't think my words get jumbled up or anything like that. I hope I'm making sense right now? I'm trying really hard to answer your questions the right way. Does my speech sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard any other voices or sounds that other people could not hear?\n\nPatient: Um... no, just the one voice. I really haven't heard anything else, I promise. It's just the one man's voice telling me what they're planning. I've never heard music or other people talking or... or sounds that aren't there. \n\nI really try to pay close attention to my surroundings at the garage, you know, to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying safe around the cars. So I think I would notice if there were other things. I hope it's okay that it's just the one voice? I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to give you the exact right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other experiences like seeing things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I've never seen anything that wasn't really there. I know some people see things, like... shadows or whatever, but I don't. My vision is perfectly fine. I have to be really careful when I'm working under the cars, looking for leaks and checking the brakes and everything, so I always make sure my eyes are focused on what's real. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm... you know, totally losing my grip on reality or anything. It's just the cameras and the voice, that's all. I haven't seen any visions or anything strange. I hope that's a good sign? I really want to be doing well, or at least... not doing as badly as I could be. Does it help that I don't see things?\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms made it harder for you to work or take care of your usual responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try my hardest not to let it affect anything. I want to be a good employee and a good wife, I really do. But... I guess it has been a little tough lately. \n\nI might have... um, I've made a few little mistakes at the shop. Just small things, really! But my boss, he... he pulled me off the high-voltage EV work. He said people were worried I wasn't being safe, which... I mean, I would never want to put anyone in danger, ever. I try so hard to be careful. \n\nAnd at home... well, my husband and I have been arguing a bit more than usual. I hate fighting with him, it makes me feel so awful. It actually got... it got a little bad, and he was the one who brought me in for an emergency evaluation. I didn't want to make a fuss, but he insisted. I hope I'm not sounding like a failure or anything? I'm really trying to get back on track and be responsible again. Do you think I can get back to normal soon?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling emotionally flat or showing less expression than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm completely flat, you know? I still care about things, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just totally shut down or... or lazy. \n\nBut... I guess there are some days, or maybe stretches of days, where I just... I don't know, I just can't seem to muster up the energy to show it? Like, I'll realize I haven't called anyone back in a while. And I used to always help run the church potlucks—I loved doing that, making sure everyone had what they needed—but lately, I've just been skipping them. \n\nSometimes I just sit in the house, or even at work in the bay, just... staring at my tools. It's like I can't make myself care about any of it in those moments. I know that sounds terrible, and I feel so guilty about it. I should be more involved, right? I'm really trying to snap out of it and be more... present. Is it normal to have periods like that where you just sort of fade out a little bit? I hope it doesn't mean I'm getting worse.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times when the voice or beliefs about being tracked continued even when your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I mean, I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely out of touch all the time, because I'm not. Does my speech sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard any other voices or sounds that other people could not hear?\n\nPatient: Um... no, just the one voice. I've never heard music or other people talking or... or sounds that aren't there. \n\nI really try to pay close attention to my surroundings at the garage, you know, to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying safe around the cars. I really hope I should have come in sooner, please don't be mad at me for waiting. I just kept thinking it would go away if I worked harder or got more sleep, you know? I just didn't want to make a fuss or cause any trouble for anyone, I promise.\n\nDoctor: During these ten months, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt unusually depressed or unusually high/energized?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I've just been working at the shop, trying to keep up with everything. I haven't had any times where I couldn't get out of bed for weeks or anything like that. And I definitely haven't been acting... you know, wild or overly energized. I just try to do my job and be a good wife. I hope that's the right answer? I really haven't noticed anything like that, I promise I'm not hiding anything. Is it normal that I just feel... steady, mostly, besides the other stuff?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your thoughts becoming hard to follow or your speech becoming jumbled or disorganized?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I really try hard to speak clearly and make sense when I'm talking to people at the shop, or to my husband. I definitely wouldn't want to confuse anyone or sound... you know, strange. \n\nSometimes I might get a little distracted by... well, by the voices, or worrying about the cameras, but I really do my best to stay on track. I don't think my words get jumbled up or anything like that. I hope I'm making sense right now? I'm trying really hard to answer your questions the right way. Does my speech sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard any other voices or sounds that other people could not hear?\n\nPatient: Um... no, just the one voice. I really haven't heard anything else, I promise. It's just the one man's voice telling me what they're planning. I've never heard music or other people talking or... or sounds that aren't there. \n\nI really try to pay close attention to my surroundings at the garage, you know, to make sure I'm doing a good job and staying safe around the cars. So I think I would notice if there were other things. I hope it's okay that it's just the one voice? I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to give you the exact right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other experiences like seeing things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I've never seen anything that wasn't really there. I know some people see things, like... shadows or whatever, but I don't. My vision is perfectly fine. I have to be really careful when I'm working under the cars, looking for leaks and checking the brakes and everything, so I always make sure my eyes are focused on what's real. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm... you know, totally losing my grip on reality or anything. It's just the cameras and the voice, that's all. I haven't seen any visions or anything strange. I hope that's a good sign? I really want to be doing well, or at least... not doing as badly as I could be. Does it help that I don't see things?\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms made it harder for you to work or take care of your usual responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try my hardest not to let it affect anything. I want to be a good employee and a good wife, I really do. But... I guess it has been a little tough lately. \n\nI might have... um, I've made a few little mistakes at the shop. Just small things, really! But my boss, he... he pulled me off the high-voltage EV work. He said people were worried I wasn't being safe, which... I mean, I would never want to put anyone in danger, ever. I try so hard to be careful. \n\nAnd at home... well, my husband and I have been arguing a bit more than usual. I hate fighting with him, it makes me feel so awful. It actually got... it got a little bad, and he was the one who brought me in for an emergency evaluation. I didn't want to make a fuss, but he insisted. I hope I'm not sounding like a failure or anything? I'm really trying to get back on track and be responsible again. Do you think I can get back to normal soon?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling emotionally flat or showing less expression than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I'm completely flat, you know? I still care about things, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just totally shut down or... or lazy. \n\nBut... I guess there are some days, or maybe stretches of days, where I just... I don't know, I just can't seem to muster up the energy to show it? Like, I'll realize I haven't called anyone back in a while. And I used to always help run the church potlucks—I loved doing that, making sure everyone had what they needed—but lately, I've just been skipping them. \n\nSometimes I just sit in the house, or even at work in the bay, just... staring at my tools. It's like I can't make myself care about any of it in those moments. I know that sounds terrible, and I feel so guilty about it. I should be more involved, right? I'm really trying to snap out of it and be more... present. Is it normal to have periods like that where you just sort of fade out a little bit? I hope it doesn't mean I'm getting worse.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any times when the voice or beliefs about being tracked continued even when your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I mean, I really don't want to make it sound like I'm completely out of touch all the time, because I'm not. There are definitely times when my mood is totally fine, just... normal, you know? \n\nLike, there was a stretch a little while ago... a quieter few weeks. I was sleeping a bit more, which I know is good for me, and I wasn't rushing around making all those huge plans like I do sometimes. I was just feeling... okay. Calm. But... um, even then... I still heard that voice in the garage. And I still really felt like my husband and my boss were tracking me. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad. I was hoping that if I just got my mood settled down and rested up, all of that other stuff would just... go away. But it didn't. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not what you want to hear. Does that make things a lot more complicated? I really am trying to be honest with you so we can fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least several days when you felt unusually high, irritable, or full of energy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I guess I have had times like that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound like a difficult person, because I truly try to be easy to get along with. \n\nBut sometimes... I just get this huge burst of energy. Like, I'll be flying so high, and I genuinely feel like I could fix every single car in town, all by myself. It feels good at first, really productive! I want to be the best mechanic they have. \n\nBut then... um, well, I can get a little... on edge, I suppose. If my husband just asks something simple, like why the dishes aren't done, or if one of the other techs even slightly moves my socket set... I just sort of blow up. It's like I feel they're trying to hold me down or stop me from doing all these great things. I always feel so terrible about it afterward, I really do. I don't want to be snappy with anyone. Is it normal to have those kinds of mood swings? I really am trying to work on my temper when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any periods of at least two weeks when the voice and the belief that you were being tracked were present even though you were not having any manic or depressed symptoms at all?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I think we just talked about that a little bit, right? I hope I explained it okay before. \n\nBut, um, yes... there was definitely a time, I think it was at least a couple of weeks, where I wasn't feeling depressed at all, and I wasn't having one of those high-energy, snappy periods either. I was just... you know, having a quieter few weeks. I was actually sleeping a lot better, getting a full night's rest, and I wasn't running around trying to fix a million things or making all those big plans. I was just feeling very even. Very normal.\n\nBut... um, even during those couple of weeks... I still heard that voice in the garage. And I still really, really believed that my husband and my boss were keeping tabs on me and tracking me. \n\nI know it sounds so strange that I would still feel that way when my mood was completely fine. I'm still just so convinced that my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on me. Even when they try to be nice and show me the footage to prove there's nothing there... I just can't shake the feeling. I know I should believe them, and I really try to, I promise. I just... I still believe it's happening. Is it okay that I'm struggling to let that go? I really am trying to be reasonable about it.
